[.c-n8h,margin-top=9.44pt]
{empty}☞ The Numbers here prefix’d to the several Days, between the Twenty-first Day of
March and the Eighteenth Day of April, both inclusive; denote the Days upon

[.c-n8,margin-top=0.11pt]
which those full Moons do fall, which happen upon or next after the Twenty-first Day of
March, in those Years, of which they are respectively the Golden Numbers; and the Sun-
day-Letter next following any such Full Moon, points out Easter-day for that Year. All
which holds until the Year of our Lord 1899 inclusive; after which Year, the Places of these
Golden Numbers will be to be changed, as is hereafter expressed. [.right]##APR.##

broken and a contrite heart, O God, thou wilt

Rend your heart, and not your garments, and

forgivenesses, though we have rebelled against

[.pa.on.p1732,margin-top=4.48pt]
fore God; yet ought we most chiefly so to do, when
we assemble and meet together, to render thanks
for the great benefits that we have received at
his hands, to set forth his most worthy praise, to
hear his most holy Word, and to ask those things
which are requisite and necessary as well for the
body as the soul. Wherefore I pray and beseech
you, as many as are here present, to accompany
me with a pure heart, and humble voice, unto
the throne of the heavenly grace, saying after me:

[.rb.p1357,margin-top=12.37pt]
¶ __A general Confession to be said of the whole Congregation, after the__
__Minister, all kneeling.__

[.ini.p1729,margin-top=11.28pt]
[.caps]##ALMIGHTY## and most merciful Father,
We have erred and strayed from thy ways
like lost sheep: We have followed too much
the devices and desires of our own hearts: We
have offended against thy holy laws: We have
left undone those things which we ought to have
done; and we have done those things which we
ought not to have done; And there is no health
in us. But thou, O Lord, have mercy upon us,
miserable offenders: Spare thou them, O God,
which confess their faults: Restore thou them
that are penitent; According to thy promises de{shy}
clared unto mankind in Christ Jesu our Lord.
And grant, O most merciful Father, for his sake,
That we may hereafter live a godly, righteous,
and sober life, To the glory of thy holy Name.
__Amen.__ [.w203]##{nbsp}##B 2

pel. Wherefore let us beseech him to grant

those things may please him which we do at
this present, and that the rest of our life here{shy}
after may be pure and holy; so that at the last
we may come to his eternal joy, through Jesus
Christ our Lord.

image::blank.svg[role=spacer,pdfwidth=1pt,pdfheight=0.01pt]

[.hc,margin-top=4.47pt]
__[.sp]##MORNING## [.sp]##PRAYER.##__

[.pa.p1720,margin-top=7.7pt]
That we should be saved from our enemies:
and from the hands of all that hate us;

[.pa.p1720,margin-top=0.24pt]
To perform the mercy promised to our fore{shy}
fathers: and to remember his holy covenant;

[.pa.p1720]
To perform the oath which he sware to our
forefather Abraham: that he would give us;

[.pa.p1720]
That we being delivered out of the hand
of our enemies: might serve him without fear;

[.pa.p1720]
In holiness and righteousness before him:
all the days of our life.

[.pa.p1708,margin-top=0.3pt]
And thou, Child, shalt be called the Pro{shy}
phet of the Highest: for thou shalt go before
the face of the Lord to prepare his ways;

[.pa.p1720,margin-top=0.05pt]
To give knowledge of salvation unto his
people: for the remission of their sins,

[.pa.p1708,margin-top=0.3pt]
Through the tender mercy of our God:
whereby the Day-spring from on high hath
visited us;

[.pa.p1720,margin-top=0.54pt]
To give light to them that sit in darkness,
and in the shadow of death: and to guide
our feet into the way of peace.

[.pa.p1720]
Glory be to the Father, and to the Son:
and to the Holy Ghost;

[.pa.p1720]
As it was in the beginning, is now, and ever
shall be: world without end. __Amen.__

[.ct,margin-top=8.9pt]
¶ __Or this Psalm: Jubilate Deo.__ Psal. c.

[.ini.p1720,margin-top=8.91pt]
O Be joyful in the Lord, all ye lands:
serve the Lord with gladness, and come
before his presence with a song.

[.pa.p1716,margin-top=7.18pt]
Be ye sure that the Lord he is God; it is
he that hath made us, and not we ourselves:
we are his people, and the sheep of his pasture.

[.pa.p1701,margin-top=0.54pt]
O go your way into his gates with thanks{shy}
giving, and into his courts with praise: be
thankful unto him, and speak good of his
Name.

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.58pt]
For the Lord is gracious, his mercy is ever{shy}
lasting: and his truth endureth from genera{shy}
tion to generation.

[.pa.p1715,margin-top=0.32pt]
Glory be to the Father, and to the Son: and
to the Holy Ghost;

[.pa.p1715]
As it was in the beginning, is now, and
ever shall be: world without end. __Amen.__

[.rb.p1353,margin-top=8.28pt]

[.ini.p1715,margin-top=3.36pt]

ker of heaven and earth:

[.pa.p1721,margin-top=-8.24pt]

Who was conceived by the Holy Ghost, Born

gain from the dead, He ascended into heaven,
And sitteth on the right hand of God the Fa{shy}
ther Almighty; From thence he shall come to
judge the quick and the dead.

[.pa.p1715]
I believe in the Holy Ghost; The holy Ca{shy}
tholick Church; The Communion of Saints;

[.pa.on.p1722,margin-top=7.72pt]
The Forgiveness of sins; The Resurrection of
the body, And the Life everlasting. __Amen.__

[.rb.p1357,margin-top=4.4pt]
¶ __And after that, these Prayers following, all devoutly kneeling; the__
__Minister first pronouncing with a loud voice,__

[.in50,margin-top=2.85pt]
The Lord be with you:

[.in50,margin-top=0.15pt]
__Answ.__ And with thy spirit.

[.in34,margin-top=-0.33pt]
¶ __Minister.__ Let us pray.

[.in50,margin-top=0.15pt]
Lord, have mercy upon us.

[.in59,margin-top=-0.09pt]
__Christ, have mercy upon us.__

[.in50,margin-top=-0.09pt]
Lord, have mercy upon us.

[.rb.p1357,margin-top=4.12pt]
¶ __Then the Minister, Clerks, and People shall say the Lord’s Prayer__
__with a loud voice.__

[.ini.p1726,margin-top=3.12pt]
[.caps]##OUR## Father, which art in Heaven, Hal{shy}
lowed be thy Name; Thy kingdom come;
Thy will be done in earth, As it is in hea{shy}
ven: Give us this day our daily bread; And
forgive us our trespasses, as we forgive them
that trespass against us; And lead us not in{shy}
to temptation, But deliver us from evil. __Amen.__

[.rb,margin-top=2.58pt]
¶ __Then the Priest standing up, shall say,__

[.pa,margin-top=1.27pt]
O Lord, shew thy mercy upon us;

[.pa,margin-top=-0.09pt]
__Answ.__ And grant us thy salvation.

[.pa,margin-top=-0.09pt]
__Priest.__ O Lord, save the King;

[.pa.p1722]
__Answ.__ And mercifully hear us, when we
call upon thee.

[.pa.p1722,margin-top=0.21pt]
__Priest.__ Endue thy Ministers with righteous{shy}
ness;

[.pa,margin-top=-0.05pt]
__Answ.__ And make thy chosen people joyful.

[.pa,margin-top=-0.09pt]
__Priest.__ O Lord, save thy people;

[.pa,margin-top=-0.09pt]
__Answ.__ And bless thine inheritance.

== Leaf 59

image::blank.svg[role=spacer,pdfwidth=1pt,pdfheight=0.01pt]

[.hc,margin-top=5.31pt]
__[.sp]##MORNING## [.sp]##PRAYER.##__

[.pa,margin-top=7.3pt]
__Priest.__ Give peace in our time, O Lord;

[.pa.p1713,margin-top=0.15pt]
__Answ.__ Because there is none other that fight{shy}
eth for us, but only thou, O God.

[.pa.p1713]
__Priest.__ O God, make clean our hearts with{shy}
in us;

[.pa,margin-top=0.15pt]
__Answ.__ And take not thy Holy Spirit from us.

[.rb.p1353,margin-top=9.59pt]
¶ __Then shall follow three Collects; the first of the Day, which shall__
__be the same that is appointed at the Communion; the second for__
__Peace; the third for Grace to live well. And the two last Col-__
__lects shall never alter, but daily be said at Morning Prayer through-__
__out all the year, as followeth; all kneeling.__

[.ct,margin-top=5.09pt]
¶ __The second Collect, for Peace.__

[.ini.p1711,margin-top=3.29pt]
O God, who art the author of peace, and
lover of concord, in knowledge of whom
standeth our eternal life, whose service is per{shy}
fect freedom; Defend us thy humble servants
in all assaults of our enemies; that we surely
trusting in thy defence, may not fear the pow{shy}
er of any adversaries, through the might of
Jesus Christ our Lord. __Amen.__

[.ct,margin-top=-1.63pt]
¶ __The third Collect, for Grace.__

[.ini.p1714,margin-top=2.08pt]
O Lord our heavenly Father, Almighty and
everlasting God, who hast safely brought
us to the beginning of this day; Defend us in
the same with thy mighty power; and grant
that this day we fall into no sin, neither run
into any kind of danger; but that all our do{shy}
ings may be ordered by thy governance, to do
always that is righteous in thy sight, through
Jesus Christ our Lord. __Amen.__

[.ft,margin-top=0.78pt]
{empty} [.right]#¶ __In__#

grace of thy Holy Spirit, that he may alway
incline to thy will, and walk in thy way: En{shy}
due him plenteously with heavenly gifts; grant
him in health and wealth long to live; strength{shy}
en him that he may vanquish and overcome
all his enemies; and finally, after this life, he may
attain everlasting joy and felicity, through Je{shy}
sus Christ our Lord. __Amen.__

Duke, the Princesses, and all the Royal Fami{shy}
ly: Endue them with thy Holy Spirit; enrich
them with thy heavenly grace; prosper them
with all happiness; and bring them to thine

[.caps]##ALMIGHTY## and everlasting God, who

[.ct,margin-top=7.59pt]
¶ __A Prayer of__ S. Chrysostom.

[.ini.p1724,margin-top=9.32pt]
[.caps]##ALMIGHTY## God, who hast given us
grace at this time with one accord to
make our common supplications unto thee;
and dost promise that when two or three are
gathered together in thy Name, thou wilt
grant their requests; Fulfil now, O Lord, the
desires and petitions of thy servants, as may
be most expedient for them; granting us in
this world knowledge of thy truth, and in the
world to come life everlasting. __Amen.__

[.ini.p1719,margin-top=0.32pt]
[.caps]##THE## grace of our Lord Jesus Christ, and the

[.ini.p1719]

upon us miserable sinners.

__O God the Father of heaven: have mercy upon us__
__miserable sinners.__

[.pa.p1719]
O God the Son, Redeemer of the world:
have mercy upon us miserable sinners.

[.pa.p1719]
__O God the Son, Redeemer of the world: have mer-__
__cy upon us miserable sinners.__

[.pa.p1707,margin-top=0.32pt]

miserable sinners.

__O God the Holy Ghost, proceeding from the Fa-__
__ther and the Son: have mercy upon us miserable__
__sinners.__

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=0.25pt]
O holy, blessed, and glorious Trinity, three
Persons, and one God: have mercy upon us
miserable sinners.

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=0.25pt]
__O holy, blessed, and glorious Trinity, three Persons,__
__and one God: have mercy upon us miserable sinners.__

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=-0.24pt]
Remember not, Lord, our offences, nor the
offences of our forefathers; neither take thou
vengeance of our sins: spare us, good Lord,

with thy most precious blood, and be not an{shy}
gry with us for ever.

[.ct,margin-top=0.92pt]
__Spare us, good Lord.__

the sighing of a contrite heart, nor the

our prayers that we make before thee in all our
troubles and adversities, whensoever they op{shy}
press us; and graciously hear us, that those
evils, which the craft and subtilty of the devil

nought, and by the providence of thy good{shy}
ness they may be dispersed; that we thy ser{shy}
vants, being hurt by no persecutions, may ever{shy}
more give thanks unto thee in thy holy Church,
through Jesus Christ our Lord.

[.t-prayers,margin-top=3.52pt]
__PRAYERS.__

[.ct,margin-top=4.1pt]
¶ __For Rain.__

[.ini.p1725,margin-top=4.8pt]
O God heavenly Father, who by thy Son
Jesus Christ hast promised to all them
that seek thy kingdom and the righteousness
thereof, all things necessary to their bodily su{shy}
stenance; Send us, we beseech thee, in this
our necessity, such moderate rain and showers,
that we may receive the fruits of the earth to
our comfort, and to thy honour, through Jesus
Christ our Lord. __Amen.__

[.caps]##PSAL##. viii. __Domine, Dominus noster.__

[.ini.p1712,margin-top=9.5pt]
O [.caps]##LORD## our Governor, how excellent is thy
Name in all the world: thou that hast
set thy glory above the heavens!

[.pa.p1724,margin-top=0.06pt]
2 Out of the mouth of very babes and
sucklings hast thou ordained strength, because
of thine enemies: that thou mightest still the
enemy and the avenger.

[.pa.p1724,margin-top=-0.24pt]
3 For I will consider thy heavens, even the
works of thy fingers: the moon and the stars
which thou hast ordained.

[.pa.p1712,margin-top=0.07pt]
4 What is man, that thou art mindful of
him: and the son of man, that thou visitest
him?

[.pa.p1724,margin-top=0.3pt]
5 Thou madest him lower than the angels:
to crown him with glory and worship.

[.pa.p1724,margin-top=-0.24pt]
6 Thou makest him to have dominion of
the works of thy hands: and thou hast put all
things in subjection under his feet;

[.pa.p1724]
7 All sheep and oxen: yea, and the beasts
of the field;

[.pa.p1724]
8 The fowls of the air, and the fishes of the
sea: and whatsoever walketh through the
paths of the seas.

[.pa.p1724]
9 O Lord our Governor: how excellent is
thy Name in all the world!

[.ft,margin-top=33.72pt]

[.office,margin-top=9.87pt]
__MORNING PRAYER.__

[.ct,margin-top=5.31pt]
[.caps]##PSAL##. ix. __Confitebor tibi.__

[.ini.p1716,margin-top=5.41pt]
I [.caps]##WILL## give thanks unto thee, O Lord, with
my whole heart: I will speak of all thy
marvellous works.

[.pa.p1704,margin-top=0.19pt]
2 I will be glad and rejoice in thee: yea,
my songs will I make of thy Name, O thou
most Highest.

[.pa.p1728,margin-top=0.37pt]
3 While mine enemies are driven back: they
shall fall and perish at thy presence.

[.pa.p1716,margin-top=0.06pt]
4 For thou hast maintained my right, and
my cause: thou art set in the throne that
judgest right.

[.pa.p1728,margin-top=0.07pt]
5 Thou hast rebuked the heathen, and de{shy}
stroyed the ungodly: thou hast put out their
name for ever and ever.

[.pa.p1728,margin-top=-0.23pt]
6 O thou enemy, destructions are come to
a perpetual end: even as the cities which thou
hast destroyed; their memorial is perished
with them.

[.pa.p1728,margin-top=-0.23pt]
7 But the Lord shall endure for ever: he
hath also prepared his seat for judgment.

[.pa.p1728]
8 For he shall judge the world in righteous{shy}
ness: and minister true judgment unto the
people.

[.pa.p1728]
9 The Lord also will be a defence for the op{shy}
pressed: even a refuge in due time of trouble.

[.pa.p1716,margin-top=-0.16pt]
10 And they that know thy Name, will put
their trust in thee: for thou, Lord, hast never
failed them that seek thee. [.right]#11 O#

[.pa.p1737,margin-top=-0.07pt]

[.pa.p1734,margin-top=-0.34pt]

[.pa.p1737,margin-top=-0.39pt]

[.pa.p1725,margin-top=-0.31pt]

[.pa.p1737,margin-top=-0.06pt]
16 The Lord is known to execute judgment:
the ungodly is trapped in the work of his own
hands.

[.pa.p1725,margin-top=-0.31pt]

and all the people that forget God.

[.pa.p1713,margin-top=0.3pt]

[.pa.p1737]

[.pa.p1725,margin-top=-0.31pt]
20 Put them in fear, O Lord: that the hea{shy}
then may know themselves to be but men.

[.pa.p1728]

[.pa.p1716]

ments are far above out of his sight, and

[.pa.p1765,margin-top=-0.35pt]
9 For he lieth waiting secretly, even as a lion

5 In my trouble I will call upon the Lord:
and complain unto my God.

[.pa.p1732]

ly temple: and my complaint shall come be{shy}
fore him, it shall enter even into his ears.

[.pa.p1732,margin-top=-0.25pt]
7 The earth trembled and quaked: the very
foundations also of the hills shook, and were
removed, because he was wroth.

[.pa.p1720,margin-top=-0.19pt]
8 There went a smoke out in his presence:
and a consuming fire out of his mouth, so
that coals were kindled at it.

[.pa.p1732,margin-top=0.3pt]
9 He bowed the heavens also, and came
down: and it was dark under his feet.

[.pa.p1732,margin-top=-0.25pt]
10 He rode upon the cherubims, and did fly:
he came flying upon the wings of the wind.

[.pa.p1732]
11 He made darkness his secret place: his
pavilion round about him with dark water,
and thick clouds to cover him.

[.pa.p1732]
12 At the brightness of his presence his
clouds removed: hail-stones, and coals of fire.

[.pa.p1732,margin-top=-0.25pt]
13 The Lord also thundered out of heaven,
and the Highest gave his thunder: hail-stones,
and coals of fire.

[.pa.p1732]
14 He sent out his arrows, and scattered them:
he cast forth lightnings, and destroyed them.

[.pa]

foundations of the round world were discovered

[.pa.p1735,margin-top=-0.49pt]
16 He shall send down from on high to fetch
me: and shall take me out of many waters.

[.pa.p1734,margin-top=-0.24pt]
17 He shall deliver me from my strongest e{shy}
nemy, and from them which hate me: for they
are too mighty for me.

[.pa.p1735,margin-top=-0.23pt]
18 They prevented me in the day of my trou{shy}
ble: but the Lord was my upholder.

[.pa.p1735,margin-top=-0.25pt]
19 He brought me forth also into a place of
liberty: he brought me forth, even because
he had a favour unto me.

[.pa.p1735,margin-top=-0.25pt]
20 The Lord shall reward me after my righ{shy}
teous dealing: according to the cleanness of
my hands shall he recompense me;

[.pa.p1735,margin-top=-0.26pt]
21 Because I have kept the ways of the Lord:
and have not forsaken my God, as the wicked
doeth.

[.pa.p1735,margin-top=-0.25pt]
22 For I have an eye unto all his laws: and
will not cast out his commandments from me.

[.pa.p1735,margin-top=-0.25pt]
23 I was also uncorrupt before him: and
eschewed mine own wickedness.

[.pa.p1735,margin-top=-0.25pt]
24 Therefore shall the Lord reward me after
my righteous dealing: and according unto the
cleanness of my hands in his eye-sight.

[.pa.p1735,margin-top=-0.25pt]
25 With the holy thou shalt be holy: and
with a perfect man thou shalt be perfect.

[.pa.p1735,margin-top=-0.25pt]
26 With the clean thou shalt be clean: and
with the froward thou shalt learn frowardness.

[.pa.p1733,margin-top=-0.22pt]
31 For who is God, but the Lord: or who

of war: and maketh my way perfect.

[.pa.p1733]
33 He maketh my feet like harts feet: and

[.pa.p1733,margin-top=-0.27pt]
34 He teacheth mine hands to fight: and
mine arms shall break even a bow of steel.

[.pa.p1733,margin-top=-0.25pt]
35 Thou hast given me the defence of thy
salvation: thy right hand also shall hold me up,
and thy loving correction shall make me great.

[.pa.p1733,margin-top=-0.26pt]
36 Thou shalt make room  enough under
me for to go: that my foot-steps shall not slide.

[.pa.p1732]
37 I will follow upon mine enemies, and o{shy}
vertake them: neither will I turn again till I
have destroyd them.

able to stand: but fall under my feet.

[.ini.p1716,margin-top=7.17pt]
[.caps]##UNTO## thee, O Lord, will I lift up my
soul; my God, I have put my trust in
thee: O let me not be confounded, neither
let mine enemies triumph over me.

[.pa.p1732,margin-top=-0.14pt]
2 For all they that hope in thee, shall not
be ashamed: but such as transgress without a
cause, shall be put to confusion.

[.pa.p1720,margin-top=0.08pt]
3 Shew me thy ways, O Lord: and teach
me thy paths.

[.pa.p1721,margin-top=0.24pt]
4 Lead me forth in thy truth, and learn me:
for thou art the God of my salvation; in thee
hath been my hope all the day long.

[.pa.p1720,margin-top=0.11pt]
5 Call to remembrance, O Lord, thy ten{shy}
der mercies: and thy loving-kindnesses, which
have been ever of old.

[.pa.p1720,margin-top=0.14pt]
6 O remember not the sins and offences of
my youth: but according to thy mercy think
thou upon me, O Lord, for thy goodness.

[.pa.p1720,margin-top=0.38pt]
7 Gracious and righteous is the Lord: there{shy}
fore will he teach sinners in the way.

[.pa.p1712,margin-top=0.23pt]

[.pa.p1712,margin-top=0.24pt]

[.pa.p1712,margin-top=0.48pt]

[.ct,margin-top=1.3pt]
[.caps]##PSAL##. xxvii. __Dominus illuminatio.__

[.ini.p1740,margin-top=3.48pt]
[.caps]##THE## Lord is my light, and my salvation;
whom then shall I fear: the Lord is the
strength of my life; of whom then shall I be
afraid?

[.pa.p1728,margin-top=-0.44pt]
2 When the wicked, even mine enemies,
and my foes came upon me, to eat up my
flesh: they stumbled, and fell.

[.pa.p1725,margin-top=0.12pt]
3 Though an host of men were laid against
me, yet shall not my heart be afraid: and
though there rose up war against me, yet will
I put my trust in him.

[.pa.p1730,margin-top=0.08pt]
4 One thing have I desired of the Lord,
which I will require: even that I may dwell
in the house of the Lord all the days of my
life, to behold the fair beauty of the Lord, and
to visit his temple.

[.pa.p1732]
5 For in the time of trouble he shall hide
me in his tabernacle: yea, in the secret place
of his dwelling shall he hide me, and set me
up upon a rock of stone.

[.pa.p1740,margin-top=-0.23pt]
6 And now shall he lift up mine head: a{shy}
mine enemies round about me.

[.pa.p1727,margin-top=-0.19pt]

oblation with great gladness: I will sing, and
speak praises unto the Lord.

[.ct,margin-top=9.84pt]
[.caps]##PSAL##. xxx. __Exaltabo te, Domine.__

[.ini.p1725,margin-top=10.38pt]
I [.caps]##WILL## magnify thee, O Lord, for thou hast
set me up: and not made my foes to tri{shy}
umph over me.

[.pa.p1713,margin-top=0.19pt]
2 O Lord my God, I cried unto thee: and
thou hast healed me.

[.pa.p1738,margin-top=0.13pt]
3 Thou, Lord, hast brought my soul out of
hell: thou hast kept my life from them that
go down to the pit.

[.pa.p1725,margin-top=-0.2pt]
4 Sing praises unto the Lord, O ye saints
of his: and give thanks unto him for a re{shy}
membrance of his holiness.

[.pa.p1716,margin-top=0.19pt]
5 For his wrath endureth but the twinkling
of an eye, and in his pleasure is life: heavi{shy}
ness may endure for a night, but joy cometh

[.pa.p1726,margin-top=0.32pt]
6 And in my prosperity I said, I shall never
be removed: thou, Lord, of thy goodness
hadst made my hill so strong.

[.pa.p1713,margin-top=0.16pt]
7 Thou didst turn thy face from me: and I
was troubled.

[.pa.p1713,margin-top=0.25pt]
8 Then cried I unto thee, O Lord: and gat
me to my Lord right humbly.

[.pa.p1713,margin-top=0.25pt]
9 What profit is there in my blood: when
I go down to the pit?

[.pa.p1713,margin-top=0.25pt]
10 Shall the dust give thanks unto thee: or
shall it declare thy truth?

2 Blessed is the man, unto whom the Lord

[.pa.p1705,margin-top=0.24pt]
3 For while I held my tongue: my bones

[.pa.p1692,margin-top=0.31pt]

[.pa.p1692,margin-top=0.55pt]

my sin.

[.pa.p1712,margin-top=0.52pt]
7 For this shall every one that is godly make
his prayer unto thee, in a time when thou
mayest be found: but in the great water-floods
they shall not come nigh him.

[.pa.p1705,margin-top=0.47pt]
8 Thou art a place to hide me in, thou
shalt preserve me from trouble: thou shalt com{shy}
pass me about with songs of deliverance.

[.pa.p1692,margin-top=0.55pt]
9 I will inform thee, and teach thee in the
way wherein thou shalt go: and I will guide
thee with mine eye.

[.pa.p1712,margin-top=0.28pt]
10 Be ye not like to horse and mule, which
have no understanding: whose mouths must be
held with bit and bridle, lest they fall upon
thee.

[.pa.p1705,margin-top=2.66pt]
11 Great plagues remain for the ungodly:
but whoso putteth his trust in the Lord, mer{shy}
cy embraceth him on every side.

[.ini.p1712,margin-top=7.74pt]

2 Praise the Lord with harp: sing praises
unto him with the lute, and instrument of ten
strings.

[.pa.p1712,margin-top=0.54pt]

[.pa.p1712,margin-top=0.24pt]

his works are faithful.

[.pa.p1712,margin-top=0.48pt]

[.pa.p1712,margin-top=0.24pt]

made: and all the hosts of them by the breath

[.pa.p1724,margin-top=0.42pt]

[.pa.p1712,margin-top=0.43pt]
8 Let all the earth fear the Lord: stand in

[.pa.p1712,margin-top=0.48pt]
9 For he spake, and it was done: he com{shy}
manded, and it stood fast.

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=0.2pt]

heathen to nought: and maketh the devices

out the counsels of princes.

[.pa.p1732,margin-top=-0.25pt]

[.caps]##PSAL##. xxxiv. __Benedicam Domino.__

[.ini.p1706,margin-top=10.85pt]
I Will alway give thanks unto the Lord: his
praise shall ever be in my mouth.

[.pa.p1706,margin-top=-7.76pt]
2 My soul shall make her boast in the Lord:
the humble shall hear thereof, and be glad.

[.pa.p1706,margin-top=0.49pt]
3 O praise the Lord with me: and let us
magnify his Name together.

[.pa.p1706,margin-top=0.25pt]
4 I sought the Lord, and he heard me: yea,
he delivered me out of all my fear.

[.pa.p1706,margin-top=0.24pt]
5 They had an eye unto him, and were ligh{shy}
tened: and their faces were not ashamed.

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=0.43pt]
6 Lo, the poor crieth, and the Lord heareth
him: yea, and saveth him out of all his trou{shy}
bles.

[.pa.p1706,margin-top=0.41pt]
7 The Angel of the Lord tarrieth round a{shy}
bout them that fear him: and delivereth them.

[.pa.p1706,margin-top=0.24pt]
8 O taste, and see, how gracious the Lord
is: blessed is the man that trusteth in him.

[.pa.p1706,margin-top=0.25pt]
9 O fear the Lord, ye that are his saints:
for they that fear him, lack nothing.

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=0.67pt]
10 The lions do lack, and suffer hunger:
but they who seek the Lord, shall want no
manner of thing that is good.

[.pa.p1706,margin-top=0.16pt]
11 Come, ye children, and hearken unto me:
I will teach you the fear of the Lord.

[.pa.p1706,margin-top=0.49pt]
12 What man is he that lusteth to live: and
would fain see good days?

[.pa.p1706,margin-top=0.49pt]
13 Keep thy tongue from evil: and thy lips,
that they speak no guile.

[.pa.p1741,margin-top=7.05pt]
37 I went by, and lo, he was gone: i sought
him, but his place could no where be found.

[.pa.p1741,margin-top=-0.73pt]
38 Keep innocency, and take heed unto the
thing that is right: for that shall bring a man
peace at the last.

[.pa.p1728,margin-top=-0.19pt]
39 As for the transgressors, they shall perish
together: and the end of the ungodly is,

[.pa.p1740,margin-top=-0.4pt]
40 But the salvation of the righteous com{shy}
eth of the Lord: who is also their strength in
the time of trouble.

[.pa.p1721,margin-top=-0.38pt]
41 And the Lord shall stand by them, and
save them: he shall deliver them from the un{shy}
godly, and shall save them, because they put
their trust in him.

[.office,margin-top=7.15pt]
__MORNING PRAYER.__

[.ct,margin-top=1.52pt]
[.caps]##PSAL##. xxxviii. __Domine, ne in furore.__

[.ini.p1753,margin-top=3.86pt]
[.caps]##PUT## me not to rebuke, O Lord, in thine
anger: neither chasten me in thy heavy
displeasure.

[.pa.p1741,margin-top=-0.8pt]
2 For thine arrows stick fast in me: and thy
hand presseth me sore.

[.pa.p1728,margin-top=-0.42pt]
3 There is no health in my flesh, because
of thy displeasure: neither is there any rest in
my bones, by reason of my sin.

[.pa.p1753,margin-top=-0.48pt]
4 For my wickednesses are gone over my
head: and are like a sore burden, too heavy
for me to bear.

[.pa.p1741,margin-top=-0.8pt]
5 My wounds stink, and are corrupt: through
my foolishness. [.right]#6#

[.pa.p1740,margin-top=8.5pt]
6 I am brought into so great trouble and
misery: that I go mourning all the day long.

[.pa.p1740,margin-top=-0.24pt]
7 For my loins are filled with a sore disease:
and there is no whole part in my body.

[.pa.p1740,margin-top=-0.73pt]
8 I am feeble, and sore smitten: I have
roared for the very disquietness of my heart.

[.pa.p1740,margin-top=-0.24pt]
9 Lord, thou knowest all my desire: and
my groaning is not hid from thee.

[.pa.p1740,margin-top=-0.49pt]
10 My heart panteth, my strength hath failed
me: and the sight of mine eyes is gone from me.

[.pa.p1739,margin-top=-0.24pt]
11 My lovers and my neighbours did stand
looking upon my trouble: and my kinsmen
stood afar off.

[.pa.p1730,margin-top=-0.41pt]
12 They also that sought after my life, laid
snares for me: and they that went about to do
me evil, talked of wickedness, and imagined
deceit all the day long.

[.pa.p1728,margin-top=-0.08pt]
13 As for me, I was like a deaf man, and
heard not: and as one that is dumb, who doth
not open his mouth.

[.pa.p1740,margin-top=-0.91pt]
14 I became even as a man that heareth
not: and in whose mouth are no reproofs.

[.pa.p1740,margin-top=0.48pt]
15 For in thee, O Lord, have I put my trust:
thou shalt answer for me, O Lord my God.

[.pa.p1740,margin-top=-0.97pt]
16 I have required that they, even mine e{shy}
nemies, should not triumph over me: for when
my foot slipped, they rejoiced greatly against
me.

[.pa.p1740,margin-top=-0.73pt]
17 And I truly am set in the plague: and
my heaviness is ever in my sight.

[.ft,margin-top=-2.14pt]
{empty} [.right]#18 For#

[.pa.p1721,margin-top=7.98pt]
18 For I will confess my wickedness: and
be sorry for my sin.

[.pa.p1721,margin-top=-0.24pt]
19 But mine enemies live, and are mighty:
and they that hate me wrongfully are many in
number.

[.pa.p1709,margin-top=0.31pt]
20 They also that reward evil for good, are
against me: because I follow the thing that
good is.

[.pa.p1721,margin-top=0.31pt]
21 Forsake me not, O Lord my God: be
not thou far from me.

[.pa.p1721,margin-top=0.25pt]
22 Haste thee to help me: O Lord God of
my salvation.

[.ct,margin-top=9.41pt]

[.ini.p1721,margin-top=7.23pt]
I Said, I will take heed to my ways: that I of{shy}
fend not in my tongue.

[.pa.p1721,margin-top=-7.98pt]
2 I will keep my mouth as it were with a
bridle: while the ungodly is in my sight.

[.pa.p1722,margin-top=0.25pt]
3 I held my tongue, and spake nothing: I
kept silence, yea, even from good words; but
it was pain and grief to me.

[.pa.p1734,margin-top=-0.08pt]
4 My heart was hot within me; and while I
was thus musing, the fire kindled: and at the
last I spake with my tongue.

[.pa.p1733,margin-top=-0.14pt]
5 Lord, let me know my end, and the num{shy}
ber of my days: that I may be certified how
long I have to live.

[.pa.p1731,margin-top=0.14pt]
6 Behold, thou hast made my days as it were
a span long: and mine age is even as nothing
in respect of thee; and verily every man living
is altogether vanity. [.right]#7 For#

[.pa.p1729,margin-top=7.61pt]
7 For man walketh in a vain shadow, and
disquieteth himself in vain: he heapeth up rich{shy}
es, and cannot tell who shall gather them.

[.pa.p1741,margin-top=-0.42pt]
8 And now, Lord, what is my hope: truly
my hope is even in thee.

[.pa.p1741,margin-top=-0.23pt]
9 Deliver me from all mine offences: and
make me not a rebuke unto the foolish.

[.pa.p1741,margin-top=-0.48pt]
10 I became dumb, and opened not my
mouth: for it was thy doing.

[.pa.p1741]
11 Take thy plague away from me: I am
even consumed by means of thy heavy hand.

[.pa.p1724,margin-top=-0.38pt]
12 When thou with rebukes dost chasten
man for sin, thou makest his beauty to con{shy}
sume away, like as it were a moth fretting a
garment: every man therefore is but vanity.

[.pa.p1729,margin-top=0.17pt]
13 Hear my prayer, O Lord, and with thine
ears consider my calling: hold not thy peace
at my tears.

[.pa.p1741,margin-top=-0.41pt]
14 For I am a stranger with thee, and a so{shy}
journer: as all my fathers were.

[.pa.p1741,margin-top=-0.24pt]
15 O spare me a little, that I may recover
my strength: before I go hence, and be no
more seen.

[.ini.p1741,margin-top=7.31pt]

12 I have not hid thy righteousness within

[.pa.p1740,margin-top=7.54pt]
14 Withdraw not thou thy mercy from me,

alway preserve me.

[.pa.p1733,margin-top=-0.7pt]
15 For innumerable troubles are come a{shy}
bout me; my sins have taken such hold upon
me, that I am not able to look up: yea, they
are more in number than the hairs of my head,
and my heart hath failed me.

[.pa.p1740,margin-top=-0.41pt]
16 O Lord, let it be thy pleasure to deliver
me: make haste, O Lord, to help me.

[.pa.p1725,margin-top=-0.41pt]
17 Let them be ashamed, and confounded
together, that seek after my soul to destroy it:
let them be driven backward, and put to re{shy}

[.pa.p1752,margin-top=-0.26pt]
18 Let them be desolate, and rewarded with

[.pa.p1728,margin-top=-0.49pt]
19 Let all those that seek thee, be joyful and

[.pa.p1740,margin-top=-0.19pt]
20 As for me, I am poor and needy: but

[.pa.p1740,margin-top=-0.24pt]

[.pa.p1744,margin-top=-0.12pt]
3 The Lord comfort him when he lieth sick

[.pa.p1744,margin-top=-0.48pt]
4 I said, Lord, be merciful unto me: heal

[.pa.p1744,margin-top=-0.73pt]

[.pa.p1735]

eth it.

[.pa.p1744,margin-top=-0.6pt]
7 All mine enemies whisper together against
me: even against me do they imagine this evil;

[.pa.p1744,margin-top=-0.24pt]
8 Let the sentence of guiltiness proceed a{shy}
gainst him: and now that he lieth, let him rise

[.pa.p1732,margin-top=-0.42pt]

[.pa.p1744,margin-top=0.06pt]
10 But be thou merciful unto me, O Lord:

[.pa.p1744,margin-top=-0.48pt]
11 By this I know thou favourest me: that
mine enemy doth not triumph against me.

[.pa.p1744,margin-top=-0.48pt]
12 And when I am in my health, thou up{shy}

[.pa.p1744,margin-top=-0.48pt]

[.caps]##PSAL##. xlii. __Quemadmodum.__

[.ini.p1744,margin-top=12.43pt]
[.caps]##LIKE## as the hart desireth the water-brooks:
so longeth my soul after thee, O God.

[.pa.p1732,margin-top=-8.15pt]
2 My soul is athirst for God, yea, even for
the living God: when shall I come to appear
before the presence of God?

[.pa.p1732,margin-top=-0.12pt]
3 My tears have been my meat day and
night: while they daily say unto me, Where
is now thy God?

[.pa.p1744,margin-top=-0.42pt]
4 Now when I think thereupon, I pour out
my heart by myself: for I went with the mul{shy}
titude, and brought them forth into the house
of God;

[.pa.p1744,margin-top=-0.48pt]
5 In the voice of praise and thanksgiving:
among such as keep holy-day.

[.pa.p1744,margin-top=-0.24pt]
6 Why art thou so full of heaviness, O my
soul: and why art thou so disquieted within me?

[.pa.p1744,margin-top=-0.24pt]
7 Put thy trust in God: for I will yet give
him thanks for the help of his countenance.

[.pa.p1742,margin-top=-0.48pt]
8 My God, my soul is vexed within me:
therefore will I remember thee concerning
the land of Jordan, and the little hill of Her{shy}
mon.

[.pa.p1744,margin-top=-0.41pt]
9 One deep calleth another, because of the
noise of the water-pipes: all thy waves and
storms are gone over me.

[.pa.p1742,margin-top=-0.47pt]
10 The Lord hath granted his loving-kind{shy}
ness in the day-time: and in the night-season
did I sing of him, and made my prayer unto the
God of my life. [.right]#11 I#

[.pa.p1727,margin-top=0.05pt]

[.pa.p1715,margin-top=0.07pt]

[.pa.p1728,margin-top=0.08pt]

eth the very secrets of the heart.

[.pa.p1731,margin-top=-0.86pt]
23 Up, Lord, why sleepest thou: awake,

[.pa.p1731,margin-top=0.23pt]

[.pa.p1731]

[.pa.p1731,margin-top=-0.26pt]

[.pa.p1736,margin-top=-0.19pt]

should be right glad.

with them that uphold my soul.

11 Wickedness is therein: deceit and guile

[.pa.p1732,margin-top=-0.06pt]

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=-0.08pt]

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=0.25pt]

ness is in their dwellings, and among them.

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=0.25pt]

[.pa.p1719]

[.pa.p1731,margin-top=0.19pt]

peace, from the battle that was against me:
for there were many with me.

[.pa.p1695,margin-top=0.31pt]

10 O trust not in wrong and robbery, give

set not your heart upon them.

[.pa.p1738,margin-top=-0.53pt]
11 God spake once, and twice I have also
heard the same: that power belongeth unto
God;

[.pa.p1739,margin-top=-0.48pt]
12 And that thou, Lord, art merciful: for
thou rewardest every man according to his
work.

[.ct,margin-top=-0.46pt]
[.caps]##PSAL##. lxiii. __Deus; Deus meus.__

[.ini.p1738]
O God, thou art my God: early will I seek
thee.

[.pa.p1727,margin-top=-8.13pt]
2 My soul thirsteth for thee, my flesh also
longeth after thee: in a barren and dry land,
where no water is.

[.pa.p1738,margin-top=0.07pt]
3 Thus have I looked for thee in holiness:
that I might behold thy power and glory.

[.ft,margin-top=0.53pt]
{empty} [.right]#4 For#

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=7.92pt]
4 For thy loving-kindness is better than the
life itself: my lips shall praise thee.

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=0.23pt]
5 As long as I live will I magnify thee on this
manner: and lift up my hands in thy Name.

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=-0.01pt]
6 My soul shall be satisfied, even as it were
with marrow and fatness: when my mouth
praiseth thee with joyful lips.

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=-0.02pt]
7 Have I not remembered thee in my bed:
and thought upon thee when I was waking?

[.pa.p1742,margin-top=0.13pt]
8 Because thou hast been my helper: there{shy}
fore under the shadow of thy wings will I
rejoice.

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=-0.36pt]
9 My soul hangeth upon thee: thy right
hand hath upholden me.

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=0.24pt]
10 These also that seek the hurt of my soul:
they shall go under the earth.

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=0.23pt]
11 Let them fall upon the edge of the sword:
that they may be a portion for foxes.

[.pa.p1670,margin-top=0.24pt]
12 But the King shall rejoice in God; all they
also that swear by him, shall be commended:
for the mouth of them that speak lyes, shall
be stopped.

[.ct,margin-top=1.63pt]
[.caps]##PSAL##. lxiv. __Exaudi, Deus.__

[.ini.p1719,margin-top=0.42pt]
[.caps]##HEAR## my voice, O God, in my prayer:
preserve my life from fear of the enemy.

[.pa.p1731,margin-top=-8.05pt]
2 Hide me from the gathering together of
the froward: and from the insurrection of
wicked doers;

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=-0.08pt]
3 Who have whet their tongue like a sword:
and shoot out their arrows, even bitter words.

[.ft,margin-top=-0.55pt]
{empty} [.right]#4 That#

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=7.65pt]
4 That they may privily shoot at him that is
perfect: suddenly do they hit him, and fear not.

[.pa.p1721,margin-top=0.23pt]
5 They encourage themselves in mischief:
and commune among themselves, how they
may lay snares, and say, that no man shall see
them.

[.pa.p1730,margin-top=-0.14pt]
6 They imagine wickedness, and practise it:
that they keep secret among themselves, every
man in the deep of his heart.

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=-0.06pt]
7 But God shall suddenly shoot at them with
a swift arrow: that they shall be wounded.

[.pa.p1731,margin-top=0.22pt]
8 Yea, their own tongues shall make them
fall: insomuch that whoso seeth them, shall
laugh them to scorn.

[.pa.p1731,margin-top=-0.42pt]
9 And all men that see it, shall say, This
hath God done: for they shall perceive that
it is his work.

[.pa.p1731,margin-top=-0.13pt]
10 The righteous shall rejoice in the Lord,
and put his trust in him: and all they that are
true of heart shall be glad.

[.office,margin-top=8.02pt]
__EVENING PRAYER.__

[.ct,margin-top=3.0pt]
[.caps]##PSAL##. lxv. __Te decet hymnus.__

[.ini.p1731,margin-top=1.57pt]
[.caps]##THOU##, O God, art praised in Sion: and
unto thee shall the vow be performed in
Jerusalem.

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=-0.32pt]
2 Thou that hearest the prayer: unto thee
shall all flesh come.

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=0.23pt]
3 My misdeeds prevail against me: O be
thou merciful unto our sins.

4 Blessed is the man whom thou choosest,

5 Thou shalt shew us wonderful things in

8 They also that dwell in the uttermost parts

[.pa.p1725,margin-top=-0.08pt]
9 Thou visitest the earth, and blessest it:
thou makest it very plenteous.

[.pa.p1714,margin-top=0.31pt]
10 The river of God is full of water: thou
preparest their corn, for so thou providest for
the earth.

[.pa.p1733,margin-top=0.49pt]
11 Thou waterest her furrows, thou sendest
rain into the little valleys thereof: thou makest
it soft with the drops of rain, and blessest the
increase of it.

[.pa.p1725]
12 Thou crownest the year with thy good{shy}
ness: and thy clouds drop fatness.

[.pa.p1714,margin-top=0.3pt]

wilderness: and the little hills shall rjoice on.

[.caps]##PSAL##. lxvi. __Jubilate Deo.__

[.ini.p1734,margin-top=7.42pt]
O Be joyful in God, all ye lands: sing praises

[.pa.p1722,margin-top=-0.06pt]
2 Say unto God, O how wonderful art thou

er shall thine enemies be found lyars unto thee.

[.pa.p1722]
3 For all the world shall worship thee: sing
of thee, and praise thy Name.

[.pa.p1722]
4 O come hither, and behold the works of
God: how wonderful he is in his doing toward
the children of men!

[.pa.p1722,margin-top=0.25pt]
5 He turned the sea into dry land: so that
they went through the water on foot; there
did we rejoice thereof.

[.pa.p1734,margin-top=-0.06pt]
6 He ruleth with his power for ever; his
eyes behold the people: and such as will not
believe, shall not be able to exalt themselves.

[.pa.p1722,margin-top=-0.06pt]
7 O praise our God, ye people: and make
the voice of his praise to be heard;

[.pa.p1722,margin-top=0.24pt]
8 Who holdeth our soul in life: and suffer{shy}
eth not our feet to slip.

[.pa.p1722,margin-top=0.24pt]
9 For thou, O God, hast proved us: thou
also hast tried us, like as silver is tried.

[.pa.p1722,margin-top=0.25pt]
10 Thou broughtest us into the snare: and
laidest trouble upon our loins.

[.pa.p1742,margin-top=-0.24pt]

[.ct,margin-top=1.33pt]
[.caps]##PSAL##. lxix. __Salvum me fac.__

[.ini.p1720,margin-top=3.59pt]
[.caps]##SAVE## me, O God: for the waters are come
in, even unto my soul.

[.pa.p1720,margin-top=-8.22pt]
2 I stick fast in the deep mire, where no
ground is: I am come into deep waters, so
that the floods run over me.

[.pa.p1696,margin-top=0.14pt]
3 I am weary of crying; my throat is dry:
my sight faileth me for waiting so long upon
my God.

[.pa.p1723,margin-top=0.61pt]
4 They that hate me without a cause, are
more than the hairs of my head: they that are
mine enemies, and would destroy me guiltless,
are mighty.

[.pa.p1708,margin-top=0.21pt]
5 I paid them the things that I never took:
God, thou knowest my simpleness, and my
faults are not hid from thee.

[.pa.p1718,margin-top=0.33pt]
6 Let not them that trust in thee, O Lord
God of hosts, be ashamed for my cause: let
not those that seek thee be confounded through
me, O Lord God of Israel.

[.pa.p1720,margin-top=0.34pt]
7 And why? for thy sake have I suffered re{shy}
proof: shame hath covered my face.

[.pa.p1720,margin-top=0.25pt]
8 I am become a stranger unto my brethren:
even an alien unto my mother’s children.

[.pa.p1720,margin-top=-0.23pt]
9 For the zeal of thine house hath even eat{shy}
en me: and the rebukes of them that rebuked
thee, are fallen upon me. [.right]#10#

[.pa.p1723,margin-top=7.74pt]
10 I wept and chastened myself with fasting:

[.pa.p1723,margin-top=0.25pt]
11 I put on sackcloth also: and they jested

[.pa.p1723,margin-top=-0.24pt]
12 They that sit in the gate speak against

[.pa.p1723]
14 Hear me, O God, in the multitude of

[.pa.p1711,margin-top=0.32pt]

[.pa.p1724,margin-top=0.07pt]

ther let the deep swallow me up: and let not

[.pa.p1724]

ness is comfortable: turn thee unto me accord{shy}
ing to the multitude of thy mercies.

[.pa.p1723,margin-top=0.2pt]
18 And hide not thy face from thy servant,
for I am in trouble: O haste thee, and hear me.

[.pa.p1723]
19 Draw nigh unto my soul, and save it: O
deliver me because of mine enemies.

[.pa.p1724]
20 Thou hast known my reproof, my shame,
and my dishonour: mine adversaries are all

[.pa.p1719,margin-top=0.27pt]

full of heaviness: I looked for some to have

[.pa.p1728,margin-top=7.04pt]
22 They gave me gall to eat: and when I
was thirsty, they gave me vinegar to drink.

[.pa.p1728]
23 Let their table be made a snare to take
themselves withal: and let the things that
should have been for their wealth, be unto
them an occasion of falling.

[.pa.p1728]
24 Let their eyes be blinded, that they see
not: and ever bow thou down their backs.

[.pa.p1752,margin-top=-0.39pt]
25 Pour out thine indignation upon them:
and let thy wrathful displeasure take hold of
them.

[.pa.p1728,margin-top=-0.37pt]
26 Let their habitation be void: and no
man to dwell in their tents.

[.pa.p1728]
27 For they persecute him whom thou hast
smitten: and they talk how they may vex
them whom thou hast wounded.

[.pa.p1728]
28 Let them fall from one wickedness to a{shy}
nother: and not come into thy righteousness.

[.pa.p1728,margin-top=-0.26pt]
29 Let them be wiped out of the book of the
living: and not be written among the righteous.

[.pa.p1728]
30 As for me, when I am poor and in hea{shy}
viness: thy help, O God, shall lift me up.

[.pa.p1728]
31 I will praise the Name of God with a
song: and magnify it with thanksgiving.

[.pa.p1728]
32 This also shall please the Lord: better
than a bullock that hath horns and hoofs.

[.pa.p1728]
33 The humble shall consider this, and be
glad: seek ye after God, and your soul shall live.

[.pa.p1728]
34 For the Lord heareth the poor: and
despiseth not his prisoners.

[.ini.p1720]
[.caps]##HASTE## thee, O God, to deliver me:

[.pa.p1721,margin-top=-8.28pt]
2 Let them be ashamed and confounded,
that seek after my soul: let them be turned back{shy}
ward and put to confusion, that wish me evil.

[.pa.p1720]
3 Let them for their reward be soon brought
to shame: that cry over me, There, there.

[.pa.p1708,margin-top=0.29pt]
4 But let all those that seek thee, be joyful
and glad in thee: and let all such as delight in
thy salvation, say alway, The Lord be praised.

[.pa.p1720,margin-top=0.11pt]
5 As for me, I am poor, and in misery: haste
thee unto me, O God.

[.pa.p1720]
6 Thou art my helper, and my Redeemer:
O Lord, make no long tarrying.

[.office,margin-top=8.34pt]

[.ct,margin-top=1.33pt]
[.caps]##PSAL##. lxxi. __In te, Domine, speravi.__

[.ini.p1735,margin-top=3.51pt]
[.caps]##IN## thee, O Lord, have I put my trust, let
me never be put to confusion: but rid me,
and deliver me in thy righteousness; incline
thine ear unto me, and save me.

[.pa.p1726,margin-top=7.28pt]
2 Be thou my strong hold, whereunto I may
alway resort: thou hast promised to help me,
for thou art my house of defence, and my castle.

[.pa.p1727]
3 Deliver me, O my God, out of the hand
of the ungodly: out of the hand of the unrigh{shy}
teous, and cruel man.

[.pa.p1714]
4 For thou, O Lord God, art the thing that
I long for: thou art my hope, even from my
youth.

[.pa.p1726,margin-top=0.35pt]
5 Through thee have I been holden up
ever since I was born: thou art he that took
me out of my mother’s womb; my praise shall
be always of thee.

[.pa.p1726]
6 I am become as it were a monster unto
many: but my sure trust is in thee.

[.pa.p1727]
7 O let my mouth be filled with thy praise:
that I may sing of thy glory and honour all
the day long.

[.pa.p1726]
8 Cast me not away in the time of age: for{shy}
sake me not when my strength faileth me.

[.pa.p1726]
9 For mine enemies speak against me, and
they that lay wait for my soul, take their coun{shy}
sel together, saying: God hath forsaken him;
persecute him, and take him, for there is none
to deliver him.

[.pa.p1726]
10 Go not far from me, O God: my God,
haste thee to help me.

[.pa.p1738,margin-top=-0.05pt]
11 Let them be confounded and perish, that
are against my soul: let them be covered with
shame and dishonour that seek to do me evil.

[.caps]##PSAL##. lxxvi. __Notus in Judaea.__

[.ini.p1744,margin-top=-0.17pt]
[.caps]##IN## [.caps]##JEWRY## is God known: his Name is great

[.pa.p1744,margin-top=-8.21pt]
2 At Salem is his tabernacle: and his

[.pa.p1744,margin-top=-0.72pt]
3 There brake he the arrows of the bow:
the shield, the sword, and the battle.

[.pa.p1732,margin-top=-0.42pt]

mighty, have found nothing.

[.pa.p1744,margin-top=0.06pt]

[.pa.p1710,margin-top=0.36pt]

[.pa.p1722,margin-top=0.3pt]

[.pa.p1710,margin-top=0.06pt]

[.pa.p1722,margin-top=0.3pt]

keep it, all ye that are round about him: bring

[.pa.p1722]
12 He shall refrain the spirit of princes: and
is wonderful among the kings of the earth.

[.ct,margin-top=10.87pt]
[.caps]##PSAL##. lxxvii. __Voce mea ad Dominum.__

[.ini.p1709,margin-top=6.57pt]
I Will cry unto God with my voice: even un{shy}
to God will I cry with my voice, and he
shall hearken unto me.

[.pa.p1722,margin-top=0.33pt]
2 In the time of my trouble I sought the
Lord: my sore ran, and ceased not in the
night-season; my soul refused comfort.

[.pa.p1722]
3 When I am in heaviness, I will think up{shy}
on God: when my heart is vexed, I will com{shy}
plain. 43

[.pa.p1722]
4 Thou holdest mine eyes waking: I am so
feeble, that I cannot speak.

[.pa.p1722,margin-top=0.48pt]

the years that are past. [.right]#6#

6 I call to remembrance my song: and in

[.pa.p1718]
7 Will the Lord absent himself for ever: and

[.pa.p1717]
8 Is his mercy clean gone for ever: and is

[.pa.p1597,margin-top=0.88pt]

will he shut up his loving-kindness in displea{shy}
sure?

10 And I said, It is mine own infirmity: but

[.pa.p1718]

and call to mind thy wonders of old time.

[.pa.p1718]
12 I will think also of all thy works: and
my talking shall be of thy doings.

[.pa.p1718]
13 Thy way, O God, is holy: who is so
great a God as our God?

[.pa.p1718]
14 Thou art the God that doest wonders:
and hast declared thy power among the peo{shy}
ple.

[.pa.p1718,margin-top=-0.06pt]

even the sons of Jacob and Joseph.

[.pa.p1609,margin-top=0.78pt]

were troubled.

[.pa.p1718,margin-top=2.97pt]
17 The clouds poured out water, the air

[.ct,margin-top=6.85pt]
[.caps]##PSAL##. lxxviii. __Attendite, popule.__

[.ini.p1717,margin-top=3.56pt]
[.caps]##HEAR## my law, O my people: incline
your ears unto the words of my mouth.

[.pa.p1717,margin-top=-7.73pt]
2 I will open my mouth in a parable: I will
declare hard sentences of old;

[.pa.p1717]
3 Which we have heard and known: and
such as our fathers have told us;

[.pa.p1717,margin-top=-0.24pt]
4 That we should not hide them from the
children of the generations to come: but to
shew the honour of the Lord, his mighty and
wonderful works that he hath done.

[.pa.p1730,margin-top=-0.07pt]
5 He made a covenant with Jacob, and gave
Israel a law: which he commanded our forefa{shy}
thers to teach their children;

[.pa.p1717,margin-top=-0.31pt]
6 That their posterity might know it: and
the children which were yet unborn;

[.pa.p1717]
7 To the intent that when they came up:
they might shew their children the same;

[.pa,margin-top=-0.07pt]
8 That they might put their trust in God:

[.pa.p1727]
9 And not to be as their forefathers, a faith{shy}
less and stubborn generation: a generation
that set not their heart aright, and whose spi{shy}
rit cleaveth not stedfastly unto God;

[.pa.p1725,margin-top=-0.34pt]
10 Like as the children of Ephraim: who
being harnessed, and carrying bows, turned
themselves back in the day of battle.

[.pa.p1725]
11 They kept not the covenant of God: and
would not walk in his law;

[.pa.p1725,margin-top=0.24pt]
12 But forgat what he had done: and the
wonderful works that he had shewed for them.

[.pa.p1749,margin-top=-0.37pt]
13 Marvellous things did he in the sight of
our forefathers, in the land of Egypt: even
in the field of Zoan.

[.pa.p1737,margin-top=-0.42pt]
14 He divided the sea, and let them go
through: he made the waters to stand on an
heap.

[.pa.p1737,margin-top=-0.37pt]
15 In the day-time also he led them with a
cloud: and all the night through with a light
of fire.

[.pa.p1725,margin-top=-0.06pt]
16 He clave the hard rocks in the wilder{shy}
ness: and gave them drink thereof, as it had
been out of the great depth.

[.pa.p1725,margin-top=-0.25pt]
17 He brought waters out of the stony rock:
so that it gushed out like the rivers.

[.pa.p1725]
18 Yet for all this they sinned more against
him: and provoked the most Highest in the
wilderness.

[.pa.p1726,margin-top=7.28pt]
19 They tempted God in their hearts:, and
required meat for their lust.

[.pa.p1726]
20 They spake against God also, saying:
Shall God prepare a table in the wilderness?

[.pa.p1729,margin-top=0.23pt]
21 He smote the stony rock indeed, that the
waters gushed out, and the streams flowed
withal: but can he give bread also, or provide
flesh for his people?

[.pa.p1738,margin-top=-0.39pt]
22 When the Lord heard this, he was wroth:
so the fire was kindled in Jacob, and there
came up heavy displeasure against Israel;

[.pa.p1726,margin-top=-0.54pt]
23 Because they believed not in God: and
put not their trust in his help.

[.pa.p1726,margin-top=0.25pt]
24 So he commanded the clouds above: and
opened the doors of heaven.

[.pa.p1726]
25 He rained down Manna also upon them
for to eat: and gave them food from heaven.

[.pa.p1726]
26 So man did eat Angels food: for he sent
them meat enough.

[.pa.p1726]
27 He caused the east-wind to blow under
heaven: and through his power he brought in
the south-west-wind.

[.pa.p1726,margin-top=-0.22pt]
28 He rained flesh upon them as thick as
dust: and feathered fowls like as the sand of
the sea.

[.pa.p1726,margin-top=0.25pt]
29 He let it fall among their tents: even
round about their habitation.

[.pa.p1726,margin-top=-0.24pt]
30 So they did eat and were well filled; for
he gave them their own desire: they were not
disappointed of their lust.

[.ft,margin-top=-0.76pt]
{empty} [.right]#31 But#

[.pa.p1734,margin-top=6.99pt]
31 But while the meat was yet in their
mouths, the heavy wrath of God came upon
them, and slew the wealthiest of them: yea,
and smote down the chosen men that were in
Israel.

[.pa.p1726,margin-top=-0.1pt]
32 But for all this they sinned yet more: and
believed not his wondrous works.

[.pa.p1726]
33 Therefore their days did he consume in
vanity: and their years in trouble.

[.pa.p1738,margin-top=-0.05pt]
34 When he slew them, they sought him:
and turned them early, and enquired after
God.

[.pa.p1738,margin-top=-0.35pt]
35 And they remembered that God was their
strength: and that the high God was their
Redeemer.

[.pa.p1738,margin-top=-0.36pt]
36 Nevertheless, they did but flatter him
with their mouth: and dissembled with him in
their tongue.

[.pa.p1726,margin-top=-0.05pt]
37 For their heart was not whole with him:
neither continued they stedfast in his covenant.

[.pa.p1726]
38 But he was so merciful, that he forgave
their misdeeds: and destroyed them not.

[.pa.p1738,margin-top=-0.29pt]
39 Yea, many a time turned he his wrath
away: and would not suffer his whole displea{shy}
sure to arise.

[.pa.p1726,margin-top=-0.29pt]
40 For he considered that they were but
flesh: and that they were even a wind that
passeth away, and cometh not again.

[.pa.p1726]
41 Many a time did they provoke him in the
wilderness: and grieved him in the desert.

[.ft,margin-top=-0.27pt]
[.w280]##{nbsp}##E e 4 [.right]#42 They#

[.pa.p1726]

50 He cast upon them the furiousness of his
wrath, anger, displeasure, and trouble: and
sent evil angels among them.

[.pa.p1713]
51 He made a way to his indignation, and
spared not their soul from death: but gave
their life over to the pestilence;

[.pa.p1725,margin-top=0.12pt]

the most principal and mightiest in the dwell{shy}
ings of Ham.

[.pa.p1713,margin-top=0.07pt]

derness like a flock.

[.pa.p1722]

[.pa.p1722]

[.pa.p1722]

[.pa.p1721,margin-top=0.2pt]

[.pa.p1733,margin-top=-0.05pt]
73 So he fed them with a faithful and true
heart: and ruled them prudently with all his
power.

[.pa.p1749,margin-top=-0.37pt]

[.pa.p1725,margin-top=-0.63pt]

[.pa.p1724]

[.pa.p1724,margin-top=0.23pt]

[.pa.p1722]

ers come before thee: according to the great{shy}

[.pa.p1712,margin-top=0.39pt]
13 And for the blasphemy wherewith our

som. [.right]#14 So#

[.ini.p1684,margin-top=8.45pt]

[.pa.p1701,margin-top=0.25pt]
3 Turn us again, O God: shew the light
of thy countenance, and we shall be whole.

[.pa.p1701,margin-top=0.49pt]
4 O Lord God of hosts: how long wilt thou
be angry with thy people that prayeth?

[.pa.p1701,margin-top=0.74pt]
5 Thou feedest them with the bread of tears:
and givest them plenteousness of tears to drink.

[.pa.p1701,margin-top=0.24pt]
6 Thou hast made us a very strife unto our

[.pa.p1689,margin-top=0.8pt]
7 Turn us again, thou God of hosts: Shew
the light of thy countenance, and we shall be

[.pa.p1701,margin-top=1.03pt]
8 Thou hast brought a vine out of Egypt:
thou hast cast out the heathen, and planted it.

[.pa.p1701,margin-top=0.49pt]

had taken root, it filled the land.

[.pa.p1701,margin-top=0.49pt]

goodly cedar-trees.

[.pa.p1736,margin-top=-0.06pt]

[.pa.p1724]
13 The wild boar out of the wood doth root

[.pa.p1724]

[.pa.p1712,margin-top=0.26pt]

nance.

[.pa.p1724,margin-top=0.3pt]

[.pa.p1724]

[.ct]
[.caps]##PSAL##. lxxxi. __Exultate Deo.__

[.ini.p1724,margin-top=0.18pt]
[.caps]##SING## ye merrily unto God our strength:
make a cheerful noise unto the God of

our horns.

[.pa.p1722]
19 For the Lord is our defence: the Holy
One of Israel is our King.

[.pa.p1722,margin-top=0.24pt]
20 Thou spakest sometime in visions unto
thy saints, and saidst: I have laid help upon
one that is mighty, I have exalted one cho{shy}
sen out of the people.

[.pa.p1722]
21 I have found David my servant: with my
holy oil have I anointed him.

[.pa.p1722,margin-top=0.24pt]
22 My hand shall hold him fast: and my
arm shall strengthen him.

[.pa.p1710,margin-top=0.06pt]
23 The enemy shall not be able to do him
violence: the son of wickedness shall not hurt
him.

[.pa.p1722,margin-top=0.55pt]
24 I will smite down his foes before his face:
and plague them that hate him.

[.pa.p1722,margin-top=0.24pt]
25 My truth also and my mercy shall be with
him: and in my Name shall his horn be exalted.

[.pa.p1722]
26 I will set his dominion also in the sea:
and his right hand in the floods.

[.pa.p1722]
27 He shall call me, Thou art my Father:
my God, and my strong salvation.

[.pa.p1722]
28 And I will make him my first-born: high{shy}
er than the kings of the earth. [.right]#29 My#

[.pa.p1753,margin-top=7.74pt]
29 My mercy will I keep for him for ever{shy}
more: and my covenant shall stand fast with
him.

[.pa.p1717,margin-top=-0.71pt]
30 His seed also will I make to endure for
ever: and his throne as the days of heaven.

[.pa.p1717,margin-top=0.29pt]
31 But if his children forsake my law: and
walk not in my judgments;

[.pa.p1729,margin-top=-0.05pt]
32 If they break my statutes, and keep not
my commandments: I will visit their offences
with the rod, and their sin with scourges.

[.pa.p1753,margin-top=-0.72pt]
33 Nevertheless, my loving-kindness will I
not utterly take from him: nor suffer my truth
to fail.

[.pa.p1718,margin-top=-0.42pt]
34 My covenant will I not break, nor alter the
thing that is gone out of my lips: I have sworn
once by my holiness, that I will not fail David.

[.pa.p1717]
35 His seed shall endure for ever: and his
seat is like as the sun before me.

[.pa.p1717,margin-top=0.22pt]
36 He shall stand fast for evermore as the
moon: and as the faithful witness in heaven.

[.pa.p1717]
37 But thou hast abhorred and forsaken
thine Anointed: and art displeased at him.

[.pa.p1717,margin-top=0.25pt]
38 Thou hast broken the covenant of thy
servant: and cast his crown to the ground.

[.pa.p1717,margin-top=0.24pt]
39 Thou hast overthrown all his hedges:
and broken down his strong holds.

[.pa.p1717]
40 All they that go by spoil him: and he is
become a reproach to his neighbours.

[.pa.p1717,margin-top=0.25pt]
41 Thou hast set up the right hand of his
enemies: and made all his adversaries to re{shy}
joice. [.right]#42 Thou#

[.pa.p1718,margin-top=7.68pt]
42 Thou hast taken away the edge of his

[.pa.p1718]
43 Thou hast put out his glory: and cast
his throne down to the ground.

[.pa.p1718]
44 The days of his youth hast thou shorten{shy}
ed: and covered him with dishonour.

[.pa.p1718]
45 Lord, how long wilt thou hide thyself,

wherefore hast thou made all men for nought?

[.pa.p1718]
47 What man is he that liveth, and shall
not see death: and shall he deliver his soul
from the hand of hell?

[.pa.p1718,margin-top=0.22pt]

es: which thou swarest unto David in thy truth?

[.pa.p1730,margin-top=-0.31pt]
49 Remember, Lord, the rebuke that thy
servants have: and how I do bear in my bo{shy}
som the rebukes of many people;

[.pa.p1730,margin-top=-0.12pt]
50 Wherewith thine enmies have blasphem{shy}
ed thee, and slandered the footsteps of thine
Anointed: Praised be the Lord for evermore.
Amen, and Amen.

[.caps]##LORD##, thou hast been our refuge: from

[.pa.p1721,margin-top=6.74pt]
3 Thou turnest man to destruction: again
thou sayest, Come again, ye children of men.

[.pa.p1709,margin-top=0.05pt]
4 For a thousand years in thy sight are but
as yesterday: seeing that is past as a watch in
the night.

[.pa.p1708,margin-top=0.36pt]
5 As soon as thou scatterest them, they are
even as a sleep: and fade away suddenly like
the grass.

[.pa.p1709,margin-top=0.36pt]
6 In the morning it is green, and groweth
up: but in the evening it is cut down, dried
up, and withered.

[.pa.p1721,margin-top=0.29pt]
7 For we consume away in thy displeasure:
and are afraid at thy wrathful indignation.

[.pa.p1721]
8 Thou hast set our misdeeds before thee:
and our secret sins in the light of thy counte{shy}
nance.

[.pa.p1733,margin-top=-0.08pt]
9 For when thou art angry, all our days are
gone: we bring our years to an end, as it
were a tale that is told.

[.pa.p1721,margin-top=-0.07pt]
10 The days of our age are threescore years
and ten; and though men be so strong, that
they come to fourscore years: yet is their
strength then but labour and sorrow; so soon
passeth it away, and we are gone.

[.pa.p1720,margin-top=1.93pt]
11 But who regardeth the power of thy
wrath: for even thereafter as a man feareth,
so is thy displeasure.

[.pa.p1721,margin-top=0.25pt]
12 So teach us to number our days: that
we may apply our hearts unto wisdom.

[.pa.p1721,margin-top=-0.25pt]
13 Turn thee again, O Lord, at the last: and
be gracious unto thy servants. [.right]#14 O#

[.pa.p1728,margin-top=-0.18pt]
2 I will say unto the Lord, Thou art my
hope, and my strong hold: my God, in him
will I trust.

[.pa.p1728]
3 For he shall deliver thee from the snare of
the hunter: and from the noisom pestilence.

[.pa.p1735,margin-top=-0.06pt]
4 He shall defend thee under his wings, and
thou shalt be safe under his feathers: his faith{shy}
fulness and truth shall be thy shield and buck{shy}
ler.

[.pa.p1728]
5 Thou shalt not be afraid for any terror by
night: nor for the arrow that flieth by day;

[.pa.p1727]
6 For the pestilence that walketh in dark{shy}
ness: nor for the sickness that destroyeth in
the noon-day. [.right]#7 A#

[.pa.p1714,margin-top=0.43pt]
30 When thou lettest thy breath go forth,
they shall be made: and thou shalt renew the
face of the earth.

[.pa.p1713,margin-top=0.1pt]
31 The glorious Majesty of the Lord shall
endure for ever: the Lord shall rejoice in his
works.

[.pa.p1604,margin-top=0.69pt]
32 The earth shall tremble at the look of
him: if he do but touch the hills, they shall
smoke.

[.pa.p1702,margin-top=3.15pt]
33 I will sing unto the Lord as long as I live:
I will praise my God while I have my being.

[.pa.p1702,margin-top=0.47pt]
34 And so shall my words please him: my
joy shall be in the Lord.

[.pa.p1716,margin-top=0.16pt]
35 As for sinners, they shall be consumed
out of the earth, and the ungodly shall come
to an end: praise thou the Lord, O my soul,
praise the Lord.

[.office,margin-top=8.72pt]
__MORNING PRAYER.__

[.ct,margin-top=1.63pt]
[.caps]##PSAL##. cv. __Confitemini Domino.__

[.ini.p1701,margin-top=3.25pt]
O Give thanks unto the Lord, and call up{shy}
on his Name: tell the people what
things he hath done. [.right]#2 O#

[.pa.p1736,margin-top=8.4pt]
2 O let your songs be of him, and praise
him: and let your talking be of all his won{shy}
drous works.

[.pa.p1748,margin-top=-0.44pt]
3 Rejoice in his holy Name: let the heart
of them rejoice that seek the Lord.

[.pa.p1748,margin-top=-0.73pt]
4 Seek the Lord and his strength: seek his
face evermore.

[.pa.p1748,margin-top=-0.5pt]
5 Remember the marvellous works that he
hath done: his wonders, and the judgments
of his mouth;

[.pa.p1748,margin-top=-0.73pt]
6 O ye seed of Abraham his servant: ye
children of Jacob his chosen.

[.pa.p1748,margin-top=-0.49pt]
7 He is the Lord our God: his judgments
are in all the world.

[.pa.p1748,margin-top=-0.5pt]
8 He hath been alway mindful of his cove{shy}
nant and promise: that he made to a thou{shy}
sand generations;

[.pa.p1748,margin-top=-0.49pt]
9 Even the covenant that he made with Abra{shy}
ham: and the oath that he sware unto Isaac;

[.pa.p1748,margin-top=-0.74pt]
10 And appointed the same unto Jacob for a
law: and to Israel for an everlasting testament;

[.pa.p1748,margin-top=-0.24pt]
11 Saying, Unto thee will I give the land
of Canaan: the lot of your inheritance.

[.pa.p1748,margin-top=-0.5pt]
12 When there were yet but a few of them:
and they strangers in the land;

[.pa.p1748,margin-top=-0.49pt]
13 What time as they went from one nation to
another: from one kingdom to another people;

[.pa.p1748,margin-top=-0.49pt]
14 He suffered no man to do them wrong:
but reproved even kings for their sakes;

[.ft,margin-top=16.34pt]

[.pa.p1725,margin-top=7.51pt]
15 Touch not mine Anointed: and do my
prophets no harm.

[.pa.p1725,margin-top=-0.23pt]
16 Moreover, he called for a dearth upon the
land: and destroyed all the provision of bread.

[.pa.p1725]
17 But he had sent a man before them: even
Joseph, who was sold to be a bond-servant;

[.pa.p1725]
18 Whole feet they hurt in the stocks: the
iron entered into his soul;

[.pa.p1725,margin-top=-0.21pt]
19 Until the time came that his cause was
known: the word of the Lord tried him.

[.pa.p1725,margin-top=0.25pt]
20 The king sent and delivered him: the
prince of the people let him go free.

[.pa.p1725]
21 He made him lord also of his house: and
ruler of all his substance;

[.pa.p1725]
22 That he might inform his princes after
his will: and teach his senators wisdom.

[.pa.p1725]
23 Israel also came into Egypt: and Jacob
was a stranger in the land of Ham.

[.pa.p1725]
24 And he increased his people exceedingly:
and made them stronger than their enemies;

[.pa.p1725,margin-top=0.05pt]
25 Whose heart turned so, that they hated
his people: and dealt untruly with his servants.

[.pa.p1725]
26 Then sent he Moses his servant: and
Aaron whom he had chosen.

[.pa.p1725]
27 And these shewed his tokens among
them: and wonders in the land of Ham.

[.pa.p1725]
28 He sent darkness, and it was dark: and
they were not obedient unto his word.

[.pa.p1725,margin-top=0.05pt]
29 He turned their waters into blood: and
slew their fish.

[.ft,margin-top=0.22pt]

30 Their land brought forth frogs: yea, even
in their king’s chambers.

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.24pt]
31 He spake the word, and there came all
manner of flies: and lice in all their quarters.

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.23pt]
32 He gave them hail-stones for rain: and
flames of fire in their land.

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.24pt]
33 He smote their vines also and fig-trees:
and destroyed the trees that were in their coasts.

[.pa.p1717,margin-top=0.65pt]
34 He spake the word, and the grass-hop{shy}
pers came, and caterpillers innumerable: and
did eat up all the grass in their land, and de{shy}
voured the fruit of their ground.

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.22pt]

even the chief of all their strength.

[.pa.p1715,margin-top=0.63pt]
36 He brought them forth also with silver
and gold: there was not one feeble person
among their tribes.

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.45pt]
37 Egypt was glad at their departing: for
they were afraid of them.

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.48pt]
38 He spread out a cloud to be a covering:
and fire to give light in the night-season.

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.48pt]
39 At their desire he brought quails: and
he filled them with the bread of heaven.

[.pa.p1715,margin-top=0.42pt]
40 He opened the rock of stone, and the
waters flowed out: so that rivers ran in the
dry places.

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.66pt]
41 For why? he remembered his holy pro{shy}
mise: and Abraham his servant.

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.24pt]

joy: and his chosen with gladness;

and do righteousness.

[.pa.p1753,margin-top=-0.72pt]

favour that thou bearest unto thy people: O
visit me with thy salvation.

[.pa.p1729,margin-top=-0.36pt]

and rejoice in the gladness of thy people, and
give thanks with thine inheritance.

[.pa.p1753,margin-top=-0.6pt]

have done amiss, and dealt wickedly.

[.pa.p1736,margin-top=0.08pt]

in remembrance: but were disobedient at the
sea, even at the Red sea.

[.pa.p1778,margin-top=-0.97pt]
8 Nevertheless, he helped them for his

to be known.

[.pa,margin-top=-0.74pt]

23 So he said, he would have destroyed

dignation, lest he should destroy them.

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.1pt]

kened not unto the voice of the Lord.

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.25pt]

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.49pt]

ons: and to scatter them in the lands.

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.74pt]
28 They joined themselves unto Baal-peor:

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.25pt]

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.74pt]

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.25pt]

teousness: among all posterities for evermore.

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.49pt]

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.73pt]

[.pa.p1703,margin-top=0.25pt]

the Lord commanded them;

[.pa.p1703]

and learned their works. [.right]#36 In-#

[.pa.p1726,margin-top=0.24pt]

ble: and he delivered them from their distress.

[.pa.p1726]

ders that he doeth for the children of men!

eth the hungry soul with goodness;

[.pa.p1716,margin-top=-0.17pt]
3 I will give thanks unto thee, O Lord,

[.pa.p1728,margin-top=0.06pt]

[.pa.p1728,margin-top=-0.23pt]

and thy glory above all the earth.

[.pa.p1728]

[.pa.p1716,margin-top=0.07pt]

rejoice therefore, and divide Sichem, and
mete out the valley of Succoth.

[.pa.p1728,margin-top=0.07pt]

Ephraim also is the strength of my head.

[.pa.p1728]

pot: over Edom will I cast out my shoe, upon

[.pa.p1728,margin-top=-0.23pt]
10 Who will lead me into the strong city:
and who will bring me into Edom?

[.pa.p1728]
11 Hast not thou forsaken us, O God: and
wilt not thou, O God, go forth with our hosts?

[.pa.p1728]
12 O help us against the enemy: for vain
is the help of man.

[.pa.p1728]
13 Through God we shall do great acts: and
it is he that shall tread down our enemies.

[.pa.p1726,margin-top=-0.24pt]
2 And they have spoken against me with

without a cause.

[.pa.p1714,margin-top=0.07pt]
3 For the love that I had unto them, lo,
they take now my contrary part: but I give
myself unto prayer.

[.pa.p1726,margin-top=0.06pt]
4 Thus have they rewarded me evil for
good: and hatred for my good will.

[.pa.p1726]
5 Set thou an ungodly man to be ruler over
him: and let Satan stand at his right hand.

[.pa.p1726]
6 When sentence is given upon him, let
him be condemned: and let his prayer be
turned into sin.

[.pa.p1726,margin-top=-0.23pt]
7 Let his days be few: and let another take
his office.

[.pa.p1726]
8 Let his children be fatherless: and his
wife a widow.

[.pa.p1714,margin-top=0.32pt]
9 Let his children be vagabonds, and beg
their bread: let them seek it also out of deso{shy}
late places.

[.pa.p1726,margin-top=0.06pt]
10 Let the extortioner consume all that he
hath: and let the stranger spoil his labour.

[.pa.p1726]
11 Let there be no man to pity him: nor to
have compassion upon his fatherless children.

[.pa.p1714,margin-top=0.31pt]
12 Let his posterity be destroyed: and in
the next generation let his name be clean
put out.

[.pa.p1749,margin-top=-0.42pt]

[.pa.p1749,margin-top=-0.24pt]

[.pa.p1737,margin-top=-0.67pt]

praise him upon the strings and pipe.

[.pa.p1722]
5 Praise him upon the well-tuned cymbals:
praise him upon the loud cymbals.

[.pa,margin-top=0.2pt]
6 Let every thing that hath breath: praise
the Lord.

[.pa,margin-top=34.23pt]
__The End of the Psalms.__
